<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, September 8, 2015  
PRESENT: Robert K. Black, Jr., Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Supervisor; Jason E. 
Whiting, Vice-Chairman; and Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman were present.  
9:03 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Black.  
      CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Donna Gierst, a resident of White Mountain Lake in Show Low, addressed the Board 
regarding the White Mountain Lake County Recreation Improvement District Advisory Board.  She spoke about 
her request to the WML Adviso ry Board in September of 2013, where she approached the WML Board about 
building a private boat dock. She expressed her concerns about the advisory board’s policy regarding the property 
owner’s rights to build private boat docks. She stated she has requested that the boat dock policy be reconsidered. 
Ms. Gierst asked Supervisor Whiting to attend the next White Mountain Lake County Recreation Improvement 
District Advisory Board  meeting. Supervisor Whiting indicated that Brandt  Clark, Attorney is continuing to 
work on this issue. Joyce Hill addressed the Board thanking them for putting the Constitutio n Proclamation on 
the Board of Supervisor’s agenda for Constitution Day. Mrs. Hill indicated that September is Suicide Awareness 
and Prevention month. She provided the Board with information on suicide prevention and a veteran’s crisis card 
which included important phone numbers for assistance. 
CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: August 25, 2015 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 August, 2015 c) 
Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, July 2015 d) Clerk of Superior Court Report August 2015 e) Juvenile 
Probation Report August 2015 f) Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, August; Winslow Precinct #2, 
August; Snowflake Precinct #3, August; Kayenta Precinct #4, August; Show  Low Precinct #5, August; and 
Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, August 2015 g) Proclamation recognizing September 17 as “ Constitution Day” 
urging all residents of Navajo County to celebrate the 228 th anniversary of the signing of the Constitution h) 
Approval of 1 Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the counter in the amount of $518.25 i) Resolution Number 31-
15, approving the amendment to the 1993 Intergovernmental Agreement between Apache, Gila, Graham, 
Greenlee and Navajo Counties creating the Eastern Arizona Coun ties Organization j) Resolution Number 32- 
15, cancelling the November 3, 2015 Election for the Pinedale Domestic Water Improvement District, Sky -Hi 
Domestic Water Improvement District and Porter Mountain Domestic Water Improvement District  k) Resolution 
Number 33- 15, cancelling the election for the White Mountain Fire District l) Agreement with Elliott D. Pollack 
& Company for an Economic and Fiscal Impact Analysis of the Apache Railway Company m) Resolution 
Number 34 -15, approving the commitment of addi tional local funds as leverage for a FY2015 Community 
Development Block Grant Application n) Revised Form 1 for 2015 CDBG Application o) Revised Form 2 for 
2015 CDBG Application p) Revised Form 3 for 2015 CDBG Application q) CDBG Form 16 Implementation of 
Milestones r) Letter to Governor Ducey regarding inmate housing s) Special Event Liquor License for the White 
Mountain Firefighter Foundation on October 2 & 3, 2015 at the Pinetop Lakes Equestrian Center : Supervisor 
Thompson made a motion to authorize the Chairwoman to sign the items in the consent agenda ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Recognition of Navajo County Employees who assisted the victims of a fatal vehicle accident that 
occurred in front of the Holbrook Governmental Complex on August 10, 2015 : Chairwoman 
Whitesinger and Sheriff K.C. Clark  addressed the item. Chairwoman Whitesinger  spoke of the 
significance of the starfish pendant, which was given to each of the employees that assisted the victims of

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-08-2015 
    Page 2 of 4 
 
the fatal accident. Mariclare Hannah, Angela Baldonado, Ernest Neff, Delton Jenkins and Michael Tyler 
were recognized for their care and support to the victims of the accident, until the first responders arrived. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of a proclamation declaring September 16 through September 20, 
2015 as “Navajo County Fair Days” : Supervisor Whiting read the proclamation  celebrating the fair’s  
84th year, and the history of the fair . He thanked all of the volunteers and  those who make the Navajo 
County Fair successful and a  “jewel”. Mike Sample, President of the Fair Board,  addressed the Board 
regarding the Navajo County Fair. Mr. Sample  spoke about the partnership with Navajo County and 
thanked Supervisor Whiting  for im plementing the grandstand project. Supervisor Whiting made a 
motion to approve the proclamation declaring September 16 through September 20, 2015 as “Navajo 
County Fair Days”; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Recognition of Navajo County Employees who completed the Navajo County Fairgrounds Grandstand 
Project: Supervisor Whiting gave a presentation on the project.  Mike Sample reported on the grandstand 
improvement project and thanked Bret Starns, Director and the facilities management  team. The team 
included the following employees; Michael Sandoval, William Rannow, Joel Johnston, Jose Villarreal, 
Roger Smorong, Carlos Attakai, Tim Otterbeck, LeAnn Baker, Travis Font, and Wayne Malone. 
HUMAN RESOURCES:  Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions:  Kimberly Eavenson 
reviewed the 12 item personnel action report. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to approve the Personnel 
Action Report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICE DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session : Supervisor 
Williams made a motion  to enter into  the Navajo County Public Health Service District Board of Directors  
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
a. CONSENT AGENDA:  
i) Amendment #7 of Contract # ADHS12 -007893 with the Arizona Department of Health Services for 
Emergency Preparedness Program for July 1, 2015 to June 30, 2016 in the amount of $225,959.00: 
ii) Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Grand 
Canyon University for Practicum for Savanah Six effective September 17, 2014 to December 31, 
2015: 
iii) Memorandum of Agreement with Kayenta Unified School District from October 1, 2015 to September 
30, 2018: 
iv) Memorandum of Agreement with Holbrook Unified School District from October 1, 2015 to 
September 30, 2018: 
v) Memorandum of Agreement with Show Low Unified School District from October 1, 2015 to 
September 30, 2018: 
Supervisor Whiting made a motion to approve the consent agenda as presented; motion  seconded 
by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public 
Health Services District and Apache County Public Health Services District for the position of a full-time 
WIC Program Registered Dietitian (RD) for the Navajo County WIC Program, that is to be shared by both 
Navajo and Apache County WIC Programs : Mary Herring disc ussed the item. Supervisor Thompson 
made a motion  to approve the Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public Health 
Services District and Apache County Public Health Services District for the position of a full -time WIC 
Program Registered Dietitian (RD) for the Navajo County WIC Program, that is to be shared by both 
Navajo and Apache County WIC Programs; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-08-2015 
    Page 3 of 4 
 
c. Presentation of Accreditation progress, explanation of the MAPP process and strategies: Allison Hephner 
gave a presentation on the Healthy Communities 2020 MAPP Meetings. Mrs. Hephner indicated that this 
is a strategic approach to community health improvement. 
Supervisor Williams  made a motion  to return to regular session ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
PUBLIC FIDUCIARY: Consideration and possible appointment of Mr. George W. Baum Jr., as the honorably 
discharged veteran to oversee the final disposition of indigent veterans and their spouses: Sherry Reed discussed 
the item. Mr. George Ba um addressed the Board. Terry Hill addressed the Board regarding  the appointment of 
Mr. Baum and the issue of  veteran burials. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the appointment 
of Mr. George W. Baum Jr., as the honorably discharged veteran to oversee the final disposition of indigent 
veterans and their spouses; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
COUNTY ATTORNEY:  Consideration and possible approval of FY16 ACJC Victim Compensation Grant  
#VC-16-058: Roxanne Padilla discussed the item. Supervisor Williams thanked Ms. Padilla and Brad Carlyon, 
County Attorney for their work on the grant. Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve the FY16 ACJC 
Victim Compensation Grant #VC-16-058; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion.  
EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a proclamation declaring the month of September as “N ational 
Preparedness Month 2015 ”: Catrina Jenkins discussed the item and asked for the Board’s approval. 
Supervisor Whiting made a motion to approve the proclamation declaring the month of September as 
“National Preparedness Month 2015” ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. Supervisor Thompson introduced Lavina Williams.  
b. Public Hearing:  Consideration and possible approval of the repeal of the Navajo County Fireworks 
Ordinance 01-12 and possible adoption of a new Fireworks Ordinance, Ordinance Number 03-15: Mary 
Springer discussed the item and introduced Jim Morgan, Pinetop Fire Chief  and Brian Russell, Timber 
Mesa Fire Marshall. Chief Morgan addressed the Board regarding the fireworks ordinance. Chief Morgan 
answered questions of the Board and indicated that with this ordinance, Navajo County would mirror the 
state law. He reported that this ordinance does not change any entity to have public displays. Chairwoman 
Whitesinger opened the public hearing. There were no public comments. Supervisor Whitesinger closed 
the public hearing. Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve of the repeal of the Navajo County 
Fireworks Ordinance 01-12 and possible adoption of a new Fireworks Ordinance, Ordinance Number 
03-15; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
FINANCE: Consideration and possible adoption of the corrected FY16 tax rates : James Menlove , Finance 
Director discussed the item and indicated that there was a clerical error  in the FY16 tax rates . He requested the 
adoption of the corrected FY16 tax rates. Chairwoman Whitesinger and Supervisor Whiting expressed their 
appreciation to Mr. Menlove for his work on the budget . Supervisor Whiting made a motion  to approve the 
adoption of the corrected FY16 tax rates; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion. 
      PUBLIC WORKS:  Consideration and possible approval of Task Agreement No. 3 of the Memorandum of 
Agreement (MOA) between the Bureau of Indian Affairs, Navajo Region and Navajo County to fund one 
Equipment Operator for a 22 month period in an amount not to exceed $85,000: Eric Oscarson discussed the item. 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Task Agreement No. 3 of the Memorandum of Agreement

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-08-2015 
    Page 4 of 4 
 
(MOA) between the Bureau of Indian Affairs, Navajo Region and Navajo County to fund one Equipment Operator 
for a 22 month period in an amount not to exceed $85,000 ; motion  seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote  
unanimous approving the motion.  
      COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Black reported on District I. He spoke about the road challenges due to the rain season. He indicated 
that he and Mary Springer, Emergency Manager are working on an emergency preparedness meeting, to partner 
with the Navajo Nation. Supervisor Black indicated that information needs to be sent out to the various chapters 
in an orderly process, to insure that the funding of reimbursements  for emergency situations  can be obtained . 
Supervisor Black  expressed his appreciation to Homero  Vela, Assistant County Manager  for providing 
assistance to the various communities. Supervisor Williams reported he attended a meeting regarding the 4FRI 
initiative. He stated he is attending “Ready Navajo County” meetings with the Show Low and Heber Chambers. 
Supervisor Thompson indicated he visited with the  Rotary Club in Phoenix, regarding funding to increase  the 
capacity to help water development. Supervisor Thompson spoke of the washed out roads  in his district . He 
reported that he met with the Navajo Nation Council and briefed them on the acti vities and meetings with the 
chapters regarding transportation, within their ju risdictions, and also within the Hopi Tribe areas. Supervisor 
Thompson spoke of getting road materials for  the gravel projects in Dilkon. Supervisor Whiting reported on 
District III meetings. He stated he toured the Town of Holbrook with Mayor Bobby Tyler. He indicated they 
visited the Bread of Life Mission, and spoke of the needs of the homeless, including the need for more beds and 
assistance. Supervisor Whiting spoke of a dinner and auction, fund raising opportunity on Oct. 3rd. He indicated 
he has a can for donations to earn money for Sheriff K.C. Clark, to keep from having a pie thrown at him . 
Supervisor Whiting invited the Board to attend the ribbon cutting of the bleacher improvements  at the Navajo 
County Fairground’s, Fair Board Chambers at 404 East Hopi  Drive, in Holbrook Arizona, following the Board 
of Supervisors meeting. 
ADJOURN: At 10:47 a.m. Supervisor Williams made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
APPROVED:       DATED: September 22, 2015 
 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
